#!/bin/sh
#
# An example hook script to check the commit log message taken by
# applypatch from an e-mail message.
#
# The hook should exit with non-zero status after issuing an
# appropriate message if it wants to stop the commit.  The hook is
# allowed to edit the commit message file.
#
# To enable this hook, rename this file to "applypatch-msg".

. git-sh-setup
commitmsg="$(git rev-parse --git-path hooks/commit-msg)"
test -x "$commitmsg" && exec "$commitmsg" ${1+"$@"}
:
===== FILE: .git/hooks/commit-msg.sample =====
#!/bin/sh
#
# An example hook script to check the commit log message.
# Called by "git commit" with one argument, the name of the file
# that has the commit message.  The hook should exit with non-zero
# status after issuing an appropriate message if it wants to stop the
# commit.  The hook is allowed to edit the commit message file.
#
# To enable this hook, rename this file to "commit-msg".

# Uncomment the below to add a Signed-off-by line to the message.
# Doing this in a hook is a bad idea in general, but the prepare-commit-msg
# hook is more suited to it.
#
# SOB=$(git var GIT_AUTHOR_IDENT | sed -n 's/^\(.*>\).*$/Signed-off-by: \1/p')
# grep -qs "^$SOB" "$1" || echo "$SOB" >> "$1"

# This example catches duplicate Signed-off-by lines.

test "" = "$(grep '^Signed-off-by: ' "$1" |
	 sort | uniq -c | sed -e '/^[ 	]*1[ 	]/d')" || {
	echo >&2 Duplicate Signed-off-by lines.
	exit 1
}
===== FILE: .git/hooks/fsmonitor-watchman.sample =====
#!/usr/bin/perl

use strict;
use warnings;
use IPC::Open2;

# An example hook script to integrate Watchman
# (https://facebook.github.io/watchman/) with git to speed up detecting
# new and modified files.
#
# The hook is passed a version (currently 2) and last update token
# formatted as a string and outputs to stdout a new update token and
# all files that have been modified since the update token. Paths must
# be relative to the root of the working tree and separated by a single NUL.
#
# To enable this hook, rename this file to "query-watchman" and set
# 'git config core.fsmonitor .git/hooks/query-watchman'
#
my ($version, $last_update_token) = @ARGV;

# Uncomment for debugging
# print STDERR "$0 $version $last_update_token\n";

# Check the hook interface version
if ($version ne 2) {
	die "Unsupported query-fsmonitor hook version '$version'.\n" .
	    "Falling back to scanning...\n";
}

my $git_work_tree = get_working_dir();

my $retry = 1;

my $json_pkg;
eval {
	require JSON::XS;
	$json_pkg = "JSON::XS";
	1;
} or do {
	require JSON::PP;
	$json_pkg = "JSON::PP";
};

launch_watchman();

sub launch_watchman {
	my $o = watchman_query();
	if (is_work_tree_watched($o)) {
		output_result($o->{clock}, @{$o->{files}});
	}
}

sub output_result {
	my ($clockid, @files) = @_;

	# Uncomment for debugging watchman output
	# open (my $fh, ">", ".git/watchman-output.out");
	# binmode $fh, ":utf8";
	# print $fh "$clockid\n@files\n";
	# close $fh;

	binmode STDOUT, ":utf8";
	print $clockid;
	print "\0";
	local $, = "\0";
	print @files;
}

sub watchman_clock {
	my $response = qx/watchman clock "$git_work_tree"/;
	die "Failed to get clock id on '$git_work_tree'.\n" .
		"Falling back to scanning...\n" if $? != 0;

	return $json_pkg->new->utf8->decode($response);
}

sub watchman_query {
	my $pid = open2(\*CHLD_OUT, \*CHLD_IN, 'watchman -j --no-pretty')
	or die "open2() failed: $!\n" .
	"Falling back to scanning...\n";

	# In the query expression below we're asking for names of files that
	# changed since $last_update_token but not from the .git folder.
	#
	# To accomplish this, we're using the "since" generator to use the
	# recency index to select candidate nodes and "fields" to limit the
	# output to file names only. Then we're using the "expression" term to
	# further constrain the results.
	my $last_update_line = "";
	if (substr($last_update_token, 0, 1) eq "c") {
		$last_update_token = "\"$last_update_token\"";
		$last_update_line = qq[\n"since": $last_update_token,];
	}
	my $query = <<"	END";
		["query", "$git_work_tree", {$last_update_line
			"fields": ["name"],
			"expression": ["not", ["dirname", ".git"]]
		}]
	END

	# Uncomment for debugging the watchman query
	# open (my $fh, ">", ".git/watchman-query.json");
	# print $fh $query;
	# close $fh;

	print CHLD_IN $query;
	close CHLD_IN;
	my $response = do {local $/; <CHLD_OUT>};

	# Uncomment for debugging the watch response
	# open ($fh, ">", ".git/watchman-response.json");
	# print $fh $response;
	# close $fh;

	die "Watchman: command returned no output.\n" .
	"Falling back to scanning...\n" if $response eq "";
	die "Watchman: command returned invalid output: $response\n" .
	"Falling back to scanning...\n" unless $response =~ /^\{/;

	return $json_pkg->new->utf8->decode($response);
}

sub is_work_tree_watched {
	my ($output) = @_;
	my $error = $output->{error};
	if ($retry > 0 and $error and $error =~ m/unable to resolve root .* directory (.*) is not watched/) {
		$retry--;
		my $response = qx/watchman watch "$git_work_tree"/;
		die "Failed to make watchman watch '$git_work_tree'.\n" .
		    "Falling back to scanning...\n" if $? != 0;
		$output = $json_pkg->new->utf8->decode($response);
		$error = $output->{error};
		die "Watchman: $error.\n" .
		"Falling back to scanning...\n" if $error;

		# Uncomment for debugging watchman output
		# open (my $fh, ">", ".git/watchman-output.out");
		# close $fh;

		# Watchman will always return all files on the first query so
		# return the fast "everything is dirty" flag to git and do the
		# Watchman query just to get it over with now so we won't pay
		# the cost in git to look up each individual file.
		my $o = watchman_clock();
		$error = $output->{error};

		die "Watchman: $error.\n" .
		"Falling back to scanning...\n" if $error;

		output_result($o->{clock}, ("/"));
		$last_update_token = $o->{clock};

		eval { launch_watchman() };
		return 0;
	}

	die "Watchman: $error.\n" .
	"Falling back to scanning...\n" if $error;

	return 1;
}

sub get_working_dir {
	my $working_dir;
	if ($^O =~ 'msys' || $^O =~ 'cygwin') {
		$working_dir = Win32::GetCwd();
		$working_dir =~ tr/\\/\//;
	} else {
		require Cwd;
		$working_dir = Cwd::cwd();
	}

	return $working_dir;
}

===== FILE: .git/hooks/pre-commit.sample =====
#!/bin/sh
#
# An example hook script to verify what is about to be committed.
# Called by "git commit" with no arguments.  The hook should
# exit with non-zero status after issuing an appropriate message if
# it wants to stop the commit.
#
# To enable this hook, rename this file to "pre-commit".

if git rev-parse --verify HEAD >/dev/null 2>&1
then
	against=HEAD
else
	# Initial commit: diff against an empty tree object
	against=$(git hash-object -t tree /dev/null)
fi

# If you want to allow non-ASCII filenames set this variable to true.
allownonascii=$(git config --type=bool hooks.allownonascii)

# Redirect output to stderr.
exec 1>&2

# Cross platform projects tend to avoid non-ASCII filenames; prevent
# them from being added to the repository. We exploit the fact that the
# printable range starts at the space character and ends with tilde.
if [ "$allownonascii" != "true" ] &&
	# Note that the use of brackets around a tr range is ok here, (it's
	# even required, for portability to Solaris 10's /usr/bin/tr), since
	# the square bracket bytes happen to fall in the designated range.
	test $(git diff --cached --name-only --diff-filter=A -z $against |
	  LC_ALL=C tr -d '[ -~]\0' | wc -c) != 0
then
	cat <<\EOF
Error: Attempt to add a non-ASCII file name.

This can cause problems if you want to work with people on other platforms.

To be portable it is advisable to rename the file.

If you know what you are doing you can disable this check using:

  git config hooks.allownonascii true
EOF
	exit 1
fi

# If there are whitespace errors, print the offending file names and fail.
exec git diff-index --check --cached $against --

===== FILE: .git/hooks/pre-push.sample =====
#!/bin/sh

# An example hook script to verify what is about to be pushed.  Called by "git
# push" after it has checked the remote status, but before anything has been
# pushed.  If this script exits with a non-zero status nothing will be pushed.
#
# This hook is called with the following parameters:
#
# $1 -- Name of the remote to which the push is being done
# $2 -- URL to which the push is being done
#
# If pushing without using a named remote those arguments will be equal.
#
# Information about the commits which are being pushed is supplied as lines to
# the standard input in the form:
#
#   <local ref> <local oid> <remote ref> <remote oid>
#
# This sample shows how to prevent push of commits where the log message starts
# with "WIP" (work in progress).

remote="$1"
url="$2"

zero=$(git hash-object --stdin </dev/null | tr '[0-9a-f]' '0')

while read local_ref local_oid remote_ref remote_oid
do
	if test "$local_oid" = "$zero"
	then
		# Handle delete
		:
	else
		if test "$remote_oid" = "$zero"
		then
			# New branch, examine all commits
			range="$local_oid"
		else
			# Update to existing branch, examine new commits
			range="$remote_oid..$local_oid"
		fi

		# Check for WIP commit
		commit=$(git rev-list -n 1 --grep '^WIP' "$range")
		if test -n "$commit"
		then
			echo >&2 "Found WIP commit in $local_ref, not pushing"
			exit 1
		fi
	fi
done

exit 0
===== FILE: .git/hooks/pre-rebase.sample =====
#!/bin/sh
#
# Copyright (c) 2006, 2008 Junio C Hamano
#
# The "pre-rebase" hook is run just before "git rebase" starts doing
# its job, and can prevent the command from running by exiting with
# non-zero status.
#
# The hook is called with the following parameters:
#
# $1 -- the upstream the series was forked from.
# $2 -- the branch being rebased (or empty when rebasing the current branch).
#
# This sample shows how to prevent topic branches that are already
# merged to 'next' branch from getting rebased, because allowing it
# would result in rebasing already published history.

publish=next
basebranch="$1"
if test "$#" = 2
then
	topic="refs/heads/$2"
else
	topic=`git symbolic-ref HEAD` ||
	exit 0 ;# we do not interrupt rebasing detached HEAD
fi

case "$topic" in
refs/heads/??/*)
	;;
*)
	exit 0 ;# we do not interrupt others.
	;;
esac

# Now we are dealing with a topic branch being rebased
# on top of master.  Is it OK to rebase it?

# Does the topic really exist?
git show-ref -q "$topic" || {
	echo >&2 "No such branch $topic"
	exit 1
}

# Is topic fully merged to master?
not_in_master=`git rev-list --pretty=oneline ^master "$topic"`
if test -z "$not_in_master"
then
	echo >&2 "$topic is fully merged to master; better remove it."
	exit 1 ;# we could allow it, but there is no point.
fi

# Is topic ever merged to next?  If so you should not be rebasing it.
only_next_1=`git rev-list ^master "^$topic" ${publish} | sort`
only_next_2=`git rev-list ^master           ${publish} | sort`
if test "$only_next_1" = "$only_next_2"
then
	not_in_topic=`git rev-list "^$topic" master`
	if test -z "$not_in_topic"
	then
		echo >&2 "$topic is already up to date with master"
		exit 1 ;# we could allow it, but there is no point.
	else
		exit 0
	fi
else
	not_in_next=`git rev-list --pretty=oneline ^${publish} "$topic"`
	/usr/bin/perl -e '
		my $topic = $ARGV[0];
		my $msg = "* $topic has commits already merged to public branch:\n";
		my (%not_in_next) = map {
			/^([0-9a-f]+) /;
			($1 => 1);
		} split(/\n/, $ARGV[1]);
		for my $elem (map {
				/^([0-9a-f]+) (.*)$/;
				[$1 => $2];
			} split(/\n/, $ARGV[2])) {
			if (!exists $not_in_next{$elem->[0]}) {
				if ($msg) {
					print STDERR $msg;
					undef $msg;
				}
				print STDERR " $elem->[1]\n";
			}
		}
	' "$topic" "$not_in_next" "$not_in_master"
	exit 1
fi

<<\DOC_END

This sample hook safeguards topic branches that have been
published from being rewound.

The workflow assumed here is:

 * Once a topic branch forks from "master", "master" is never
   merged into it again (either directly or indirectly).

 * Once a topic branch is fully cooked and merged into "master",
   it is deleted.  If you need to build on top of it to correct
   earlier mistakes, a new topic branch is created by forking at
   the tip of the "master".  This is not strictly necessary, but
   it makes it easier to keep your history simple.

 * Whenever you need to test or publish your changes to topic
   branches, merge them into "next" branch.

The script, being an example, hardcodes the publish branch name
to be "next", but it is trivial to make it configurable via
$GIT_DIR/config mechanism.

With this workflow, you would want to know:

(1) ... if a topic branch has ever been merged to "next".  Young
    topic branches can have stupid mistakes you would rather
    clean up before publishing, and things that have not been
    merged into other branches can be easily rebased without
    affecting other people.  But once it is published, you would
    not want to rewind it.

(2) ... if a topic branch has been fully merged to "master".
    Then you can delete it.  More importantly, you should not
    build on top of it -- other people may already want to
    change things related to the topic as patches against your
    "master", so if you need further changes, it is better to
    fork the topic (perhaps with the same name) afresh from the
    tip of "master".

Let's look at this example:

		   o---o---o---o---o---o---o---o---o---o "next"
		  /       /           /           /
		 /   a---a---b A     /           /
		/   /               /           /
	       /   /   c---c---c---c B         /
	      /   /   /             \         /
	     /   /   /   b---b C     \       /
	    /   /   /   /             \     /
    ---o---o---o---o---o---o---o---o---o---o---o "master"


A, B and C are topic branches.

 * A has one fix since it was merged up to "next".

 * B has finished.  It has been fully merged up to "master" and "next",
   and is ready to be deleted.

 * C has not merged to "next" at all.

We would want to allow C to be rebased, refuse A, and encourage
B to be deleted.

To compute (1):

	git rev-list ^master ^topic next
	git rev-list ^master        next

	if these match, topic has not merged in next at all.

To compute (2):

	git rev-list master..topic

	if this is empty, it is fully merged to "master".

DOC_END
===== FILE: .git/hooks/pre-receive.sample =====
#!/bin/sh
#
# An example hook script to make use of push options.
# The example simply echoes all push options that start with 'echoback='
# and rejects all pushes when the "reject" push option is used.
#
# To enable this hook, rename this file to "pre-receive".

if test -n "$GIT_PUSH_OPTION_COUNT"
then
	i=0
	while test "$i" -lt "$GIT_PUSH_OPTION_COUNT"
	do
		eval "value=\$GIT_PUSH_OPTION_$i"
		case "$value" in
		echoback=*)
			echo "echo from the pre-receive-hook: ${value#*=}" >&2
			;;
		reject)
			exit 1
		esac
		i=$((i + 1))
	done
fi
===== FILE: .git/hooks/prepare-commit-msg.sample =====
#!/bin/sh
#
# An example hook script to prepare the commit log message.
# Called by "git commit" with the name of the file that has the
# commit message, followed by the description of the commit
# message's source.  The hook's purpose is to edit the commit
# message file.  If the hook fails with a non-zero status,
# the commit is aborted.
#
# To enable this hook, rename this file to "prepare-commit-msg".

# This hook includes three examples. The first one removes the
# "# Please enter the commit message..." help message.
#
# The second includes the output of "git diff --name-status -r"
# into the message, just before the "git status" output.  It is
# commented because it doesn't cope with --amend or with squashed
# commits.
#
# The third example adds a Signed-off-by line to the message, that can
# still be edited.  This is rarely a good idea.

COMMIT_MSG_FILE=$1
COMMIT_SOURCE=$2
SHA1=$3

/usr/bin/perl -i.bak -ne 'print unless(m/^. Please enter the commit message/..m/^#$/)' "$COMMIT_MSG_FILE"

#  ,|template,)
#    /usr/bin/perl -i.bak -pe '
#       print "\n" . `git diff --cached --name-status -r`
# 	 if /^#/ && $first++ == 0' "$COMMIT_MSG_FILE" ;;
#  *) ;;
# esac

# SOB=$(git var GIT_COMMITTER_IDENT | sed -n 's/^\(.*>\).*$/Signed-off-by: \1/p')
# git interpret-trailers --in-place --trailer "$SOB" "$COMMIT_MSG_FILE"
# if test -z "$COMMIT_SOURCE"
# then
#   /usr/bin/perl -i.bak -pe 'print "\n" if !$first_line++' "$COMMIT_MSG_FILE"

===== FILE: .git/hooks/push-to-checkout.sample =====
#!/bin/sh

# An example hook script to update a checked-out tree on a git push.
#
# This hook is invoked by git-receive-pack(1) when it reacts to git
# push and updates reference(s) in its repository, and when the push
# tries to update the branch that is currently checked out and the
# receive.denyCurrentBranch configuration variable is set to
# updateInstead.
#
# By default, such a push is refused if the working tree and the index
# of the remote repository has any difference from the currently
# checked out commit; when both the working tree and the index match
# the current commit, they are updated to match the newly pushed tip
# of the branch. This hook is to be used to override the default
# behaviour; however the code below reimplements the default behaviour
# as a starting point for convenient modification.
#
# The hook receives the commit with which the tip of the current
# branch is going to be updated:
commit=$1

# It can exit with a non-zero status to refuse the push (when it does
# so, it must not modify the index or the working tree).
die () {
	echo >&2 "$*"
	exit 1
}

# Or it can make any necessary changes to the working tree and to the
# index to bring them to the desired state when the tip of the current
# branch is updated to the new commit, and exit with a zero status.
#
# For example, the hook can simply run git read-tree -u -m HEAD "$1"
# in order to emulate git fetch that is run in the reverse direction
# with git push, as the two-tree form of git read-tree -u -m is
# essentially the same as git switch or git checkout that switches
# branches while keeping the local changes in the working tree that do
# not interfere with the difference between the branches.

# The below is a more-or-less exact translation to shell of the C code
# for the default behaviour for git's push-to-checkout hook defined in
# the push_to_deploy() function in builtin/receive-pack.c.
#
# Note that the hook will be executed from the repository directory,
# not from the working tree, so if you want to perform operations on
# the working tree, you will have to adapt your code accordingly, e.g.
# by adding "cd .." or using relative paths.

if ! git update-index -q --ignore-submodules --refresh
then
	die "Up-to-date check failed"
fi

if ! git diff-files --quiet --ignore-submodules --
then
	die "Working directory has unstaged changes"
fi

# This is a rough translation of:
#
#   head_has_history() ? "HEAD" : EMPTY_TREE_SHA1_HEX
if git cat-file -e HEAD 2>/dev/null
then
	head=HEAD
else
	head=$(git hash-object -t tree --stdin </dev/null)
fi

if ! git diff-index --quiet --cached --ignore-submodules $head --
then
	die "Working directory has staged changes"
fi

if ! git read-tree -u -m "$commit"
then
	die "Could not update working tree to new HEAD"
fi
===== FILE: .git/hooks/sendemail-validate.sample =====
#!/bin/sh

# An example hook script to validate a patch (and/or patch series) before
# sending it via email.
#
# The hook should exit with non-zero status after issuing an appropriate
# message if it wants to prevent the email(s) from being sent.
#
# To enable this hook, rename this file to "sendemail-validate".
#
# By default, it will only check that the patch(es) can be applied on top of
# the default upstream branch without conflicts in a secondary worktree. After
# validation (successful or not) of the last patch of a series, the worktree
# will be deleted.
#
# The following config variables can be set to change the default remote and
# remote ref that are used to apply the patches against:
#
#   sendemail.validateRemote (default: origin)
#   sendemail.validateRemoteRef (default: HEAD)
#
# Replace the TODO placeholders with appropriate checks according to your
# needs.

validate_cover_letter () {
	file="$1"
	# TODO: Replace with appropriate checks (e.g. spell checking).
	true
}

validate_patch () {
	file="$1"
	# Ensure that the patch applies without conflicts.
	git am -3 "$file" || return
	# TODO: Replace with appropriate checks for this patch
	# (e.g. checkpatch.pl).
	true
}

validate_series () {
	# TODO: Replace with appropriate checks for the whole series
	# (e.g. quick build, coding style checks, etc.).
	true
}

# main -------------------------------------------------------------------------

if test "$GIT_SENDEMAIL_FILE_COUNTER" = 1
then
	remote=$(git config --default origin --get sendemail.validateRemote) &&
	ref=$(git config --default HEAD --get sendemail.validateRemoteRef) &&
	worktree=$(mktemp --tmpdir -d sendemail-validate.XXXXXXX) &&
	git worktree add -fd --checkout "$worktree" "refs/remotes/$remote/$ref" &&
	git config --replace-all sendemail.validateWorktree "$worktree"
else
	worktree=$(git config --get sendemail.validateWorktree)
fi || {
	echo "sendemail-validate: error: failed to prepare worktree" >&2
	exit 1
}

unset GIT_DIR GIT_WORK_TREE
cd "$worktree" &&

if grep -q "^diff --git " "$1"
then
	validate_patch "$1"
else
	validate_cover_letter "$1"
fi &&

if test "$GIT_SENDEMAIL_FILE_COUNTER" = "$GIT_SENDEMAIL_FILE_TOTAL"
then
	git config --unset-all sendemail.validateWorktree &&
	trap 'git worktree remove -ff "$worktree"' EXIT &&
	validate_series
fi
===== FILE: .git/hooks/update.sample =====
#!/bin/sh
#
# An example hook script to block unannotated tags from entering.
# Called by "git receive-pack" with arguments: refname sha1-old sha1-new
#
# To enable this hook, rename this file to "update".
#
# Config
# ------
# hooks.allowunannotated
#   This boolean sets whether unannotated tags will be allowed into the
#   repository.  By default they won't be.
# hooks.allowdeletetag
#   This boolean sets whether deleting tags will be allowed in the
#   repository.  By default they won't be.
# hooks.allowmodifytag
#   This boolean sets whether a tag may be modified after creation. By default
#   it won't be.
# hooks.allowdeletebranch
#   This boolean sets whether deleting branches will be allowed in the
#   repository.  By default they won't be.
# hooks.denycreatebranch
#   This boolean sets whether remotely creating branches will be denied
#   in the repository.  By default this is allowed.
#

# --- Command line
refname="$1"
oldrev="$2"
newrev="$3"

# --- Safety check
if [ -z "$GIT_DIR" ]; then
	echo "Don't run this script from the command line." >&2
	echo " (if you want, you could supply GIT_DIR then run" >&2
	echo "  $0 <ref> <oldrev> <newrev>)" >&2
	exit 1
fi

if [ -z "$refname" -o -z "$oldrev" -o -z "$newrev" ]; then
	echo "usage: $0 <ref> <oldrev> <newrev>" >&2
	exit 1
fi

# --- Config
allowunannotated=$(git config --type=bool hooks.allowunannotated)
allowdeletebranch=$(git config --type=bool hooks.allowdeletebranch)
denycreatebranch=$(git config --type=bool hooks.denycreatebranch)
allowdeletetag=$(git config --type=bool hooks.allowdeletetag)
allowmodifytag=$(git config --type=bool hooks.allowmodifytag)

# check for no description
projectdesc=$(sed -e '1q' "$GIT_DIR/description")
case "$projectdesc" in
"Unnamed repository"* | "")
	echo "*** Project description file hasn't been set" >&2
	exit 1
	;;
esac

# --- Check types
# if $newrev is 0000...0000, it's a commit to delete a ref.
zero=$(git hash-object --stdin </dev/null | tr '[0-9a-f]' '0')
if [ "$newrev" = "$zero" ]; then
	newrev_type=delete
else
	newrev_type=$(git cat-file -t $newrev)
fi

case "$refname","$newrev_type" in
	refs/tags/*,commit)
		# un-annotated tag
		short_refname=${refname##refs/tags/}
		if [ "$allowunannotated" != "true" ]; then
			echo "*** The un-annotated tag, $short_refname, is not allowed in this repository" >&2
			echo "*** Use 'git tag [ -a | -s ]' for tags you want to propagate." >&2
			exit 1
		fi
		;;
	refs/tags/*,delete)
		# delete tag
		if [ "$allowdeletetag" != "true" ]; then
			echo "*** Deleting a tag is not allowed in this repository" >&2
			exit 1
		fi
		;;
	refs/tags/*,tag)
		# annotated tag
		if [ "$allowmodifytag" != "true" ] && git rev-parse $refname > /dev/null 2>&1
		then
			echo "*** Tag '$refname' already exists." >&2
			echo "*** Modifying a tag is not allowed in this repository." >&2
			exit 1
		fi
		;;
	refs/heads/*,commit)
		# branch
		if [ "$oldrev" = "$zero" -a "$denycreatebranch" = "true" ]; then
			echo "*** Creating a branch is not allowed in this repository" >&2
			exit 1
		fi
		;;
	refs/heads/*,delete)
		# delete branch
		if [ "$allowdeletebranch" != "true" ]; then
			echo "*** Deleting a branch is not allowed in this repository" >&2
			exit 1
		fi
		;;
	refs/remotes/*,commit)
		# tracking branch
		;;
	refs/remotes/*,delete)
		# delete tracking branch
		if [ "$allowdeletebranch" != "true" ]; then
			echo "*** Deleting a tracking branch is not allowed in this repository" >&2
			exit 1
		fi
		;;
	*)
		# Anything else (is there anything else?)
		echo "*** Update hook: unknown type of update to ref $refname of type $newrev_type" >&2
		exit 1
		;;
esac

# --- Finished
exit 0

===== FILE: Makefile =====
# Analytic App Development Makefile

.PHONY: help start-app stop-app restart-app \
        start-redis stop-redis \
        start-celery stop-celery \
        logs-all logs-app logs-celery \
        test-all test-unit test-ui \
        lint lint-css format clean

BLUE := \033[0;34m
GREEN := \033[0;32m
YELLOW := \033[0;33m
RED := \033[0;31m
NC := \033[0m

PYTHON := .venv/bin/python
UVICORN := .venv/bin/uvicorn
CELERY := .venv/bin/celery
DOCKER_COMPOSE := $(shell if command -v docker-compose >/dev/null 2>&1; then echo docker-compose; else echo $(DOCKER_COMPOSE); fi)

PID_DIR := .pids
LOG_DIR := logs
APP_PID := $(PID_DIR)/app.pid
APP_LOG := $(LOG_DIR)/app-console.log
CELERY_PID := $(PID_DIR)/celery.pid
CELERY_LOG := $(LOG_DIR)/celery-console.log

# ============================================================================
# Help
# ============================================================================

help:
	@echo ""
	@echo "$(BLUE)Analytic App Development Commands$(NC)"
	@echo "==================================="
	@echo ""
	@echo "$(GREEN)Application:$(NC)"
	@echo "  make start-app      Start the app (uvicorn with reload)"
	@echo "  make stop-app       Stop the app"
	@echo "  make restart-app    Restart the app"
	@echo ""
	@echo "$(GREEN)Infrastructure:$(NC)"
	@echo "  make start-redis    Start Redis via Docker Compose"
	@echo "  make stop-redis     Stop Redis and remove container"
	@echo ""
	@echo "$(GREEN)Celery:$(NC)"
	@echo "  make start-celery   Start the Celery worker"
	@echo "  make stop-celery    Stop the Celery worker"
	@echo ""
	@echo "$(GREEN)Logs:$(NC)"
	@echo "  make logs-all       Tail all logs (app + celery)"
	@echo "  make logs-app       Tail app logs only"
	@echo "  make logs-celery    Tail celery worker logs only"
	@echo ""
	@echo "$(GREEN)Testing:$(NC)"
	@echo "  make test-all     Run all tests (unit + ui)"
	@echo "  make test-unit    Run unit tests only (no browser)"
	@echo "  make test-ui      Run UI tests only (headless Selenium)"
	@echo ""
	@echo "$(GREEN)Quality:$(NC)"
	@echo "  make lint         Run linter"
	@echo "  make lint-css     Check for unused CSS selectors"
	@echo "  make format       Auto-format code"
	@echo ""
	@echo "$(GREEN)Utilities:$(NC)"
	@echo "  make clean        Remove PID files and temp data"
	@echo ""

# ============================================================================
# Application
# ============================================================================

$(PID_DIR):
	@mkdir -p $(PID_DIR)

$(LOG_DIR):
	@mkdir -p $(LOG_DIR)

start-app: $(PID_DIR) $(LOG_DIR)
	@if [ -f $(APP_PID) ] && kill -0 $$(cat $(APP_PID)) 2>/dev/null; then \
		echo "$(YELLOW)App already running (PID: $$(cat $(APP_PID)))$(NC)"; \
	else \
		echo "$(BLUE)Starting app...$(NC)"; \
		$(UVICORN) src.main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8050 --reload > $(APP_LOG) 2>&1 & \
		echo $$! > $(APP_PID); \
		sleep 1; \
		if kill -0 $$(cat $(APP_PID)) 2>/dev/null; then \
			echo "$(GREEN)App started (PID: $$(cat $(APP_PID))) - http://localhost:8050$(NC)"; \
		else \
			echo "$(RED)App failed to start. Check $(APP_LOG)$(NC)"; \
			rm -f $(APP_PID); \
			exit 1; \
		fi \
	fi

stop-app:
	@if [ -f $(APP_PID) ]; then \
		if kill -0 $$(cat $(APP_PID)) 2>/dev/null; then \
			echo "$(BLUE)Stopping app (PID: $$(cat $(APP_PID)))...$(NC)"; \
			kill $$(cat $(APP_PID)) 2>/dev/null || true; \
			sleep 1; \
			kill -9 $$(cat $(APP_PID)) 2>/dev/null || true; \
		fi; \
		rm -f $(APP_PID); \
		echo "$(GREEN)App stopped$(NC)"; \
	else \
		echo "$(YELLOW)App not running$(NC)"; \
	fi

restart-app: stop-app start-app

# ============================================================================
# Infrastructure (Redis)
# ============================================================================

start-redis:
	@if $(DOCKER_COMPOSE) ps --status running 2>/dev/null | grep -q redis; then \
		echo "$(YELLOW)Redis already running$(NC)"; \
	else \
		echo "$(BLUE)Starting Redis...$(NC)"; \
		$(DOCKER_COMPOSE) up -d redis; \
		echo "$(GREEN)Redis started on localhost:6379$(NC)"; \
	fi

stop-redis:
	@echo "$(BLUE)Stopping Redis...$(NC)"
	@$(DOCKER_COMPOSE) down -v
	@echo "$(GREEN)Redis stopped and cleaned up$(NC)"

# ============================================================================
# Celery
# ============================================================================

start-celery: $(PID_DIR) $(LOG_DIR)
	@if [ -f $(CELERY_PID) ] && kill -0 $$(cat $(CELERY_PID)) 2>/dev/null; then \
		echo "$(YELLOW)Celery worker already running (PID: $$(cat $(CELERY_PID)))$(NC)"; \
	else \
		echo "$(BLUE)Starting Celery worker...$(NC)"; \
		$(CELERY) -A src.celery_worker.celery_app worker --loglevel=info > $(CELERY_LOG) 2>&1 & \
		echo $$! > $(CELERY_PID); \
		sleep 2; \
		if kill -0 $$(cat $(CELERY_PID)) 2>/dev/null; then \
			echo "$(GREEN)Celery worker started (PID: $$(cat $(CELERY_PID)))$(NC)"; \
		else \
			echo "$(RED)Celery worker failed to start. Check $(CELERY_LOG)$(NC)"; \
			rm -f $(CELERY_PID); \
			exit 1; \
		fi \
	fi

stop-celery:
	@if [ -f $(CELERY_PID) ]; then \
		if kill -0 $$(cat $(CELERY_PID)) 2>/dev/null; then \
			echo "$(BLUE)Stopping Celery worker (PID: $$(cat $(CELERY_PID)))...$(NC)"; \
			kill $$(cat $(CELERY_PID)) 2>/dev/null || true; \
			sleep 1; \
			kill -9 $$(cat $(CELERY_PID)) 2>/dev/null || true; \
		fi; \
		rm -f $(CELERY_PID); \
		echo "$(GREEN)Celery worker stopped$(NC)"; \
	else \
		echo "$(YELLOW)Celery worker not running$(NC)"; \
	fi

# ============================================================================
# Logs
# ============================================================================

logs-all:
	@echo "$(BLUE)Tailing all logs (Ctrl+C to stop)...$(NC)"
	@tail -f $(APP_LOG) $(CELERY_LOG) 2>/dev/null || \
		echo "$(YELLOW)No log files found. Start services first.$(NC)"

logs-app:
	@echo "$(BLUE)Tailing app logs (Ctrl+C to stop)...$(NC)"
	@tail -f $(APP_LOG) 2>/dev/null || \
		echo "$(YELLOW)No app log file. Start app first.$(NC)"

logs-celery:
	@echo "$(BLUE)Tailing celery logs (Ctrl+C to stop)...$(NC)"
	@tail -f $(CELERY_LOG) 2>/dev/null || \
		echo "$(YELLOW)No celery log file. Start celery first.$(NC)"

# ============================================================================
# Testing
# ============================================================================

test-all:
	@echo "$(BLUE)Running all tests...$(NC)"
	@$(PYTHON) -m pytest tests/ -v --tb=short

test-unit:
	@echo "$(BLUE)Running unit tests...$(NC)"
	@$(PYTHON) -m pytest tests/ -v --tb=short -m "not ui"

test-ui:
	@echo "$(BLUE)Running UI tests (headless Selenium)...$(NC)"
	@$(PYTHON) -m pytest tests/ -v --tb=short -m "ui"

# ============================================================================
# Quality
# ============================================================================

lint:
	@$(PYTHON) -m ruff check src/ tests/
	@$(PYTHON) -m ruff format --check src/ tests/

lint-css:
	@echo "$(BLUE)Checking for unused CSS selectors...$(NC)"
	@mkdir -p .purgecss
	@npx purgecss --config purgecss.config.cjs 2>/dev/null
	@if diff -q assets/styles.css .purgecss/styles.css > /dev/null 2>&1; then \
		echo "$(GREEN)No unused CSS selectors found$(NC)"; \
	else \
		echo "$(YELLOW)Potentially unused CSS detected:$(NC)"; \
		diff assets/styles.css .purgecss/styles.css || true; \
	fi
	@rm -rf .purgecss

format:
	@$(PYTHON) -m ruff format src/ tests/
	@$(PYTHON) -m ruff check --fix src/ tests/

# ============================================================================
# Cleanup
# ============================================================================

clean:
	@echo "$(BLUE)Cleaning up...$(NC)"
	@rm -rf $(PID_DIR)
	@rm -f $(APP_LOG) $(CELERY_LOG)
	@find . -type d -name __pycache__ -exec rm -rf {} + 2>/dev/null || true
	@echo "$(GREEN)Cleaned$(NC)"
===== FILE: assets/_theme_init.js =====
/**
 * Early theme initialization — runs before React renders to prevent
 * a flash of the wrong color scheme.
 *
 * Reads the persisted preference from localStorage (key: "theme-store",
 * written by dcc.Store).  Falls back to the browser / OS preference
 * via the prefers-color-scheme media query.
 */
(function () {
    try {
        var stored = JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem("theme-store"));
        if (stored === "light" || stored === "dark") {
            document.documentElement.setAttribute("data-mantine-color-scheme", stored);
        } else {
            var prefersDark = window.matchMedia("(prefers-color-scheme: dark)").matches;
            document.documentElement.setAttribute(
                "data-mantine-color-scheme",
                prefersDark ? "dark" : "light"
            );
        }
    } catch (_) {
        document.documentElement.setAttribute("data-mantine-color-scheme", "dark");
    }
})();
===== FILE: assets/clientside.js =====
window.dash_clientside = window.dash_clientside || {};

window.dash_clientside.theme = {
    /**
     * Toggle between dark and light mode.
     *
     * Reads the currently-active scheme from the <html> attribute
     * (rather than the store, which may be null on first visit)
     * and returns the opposite value so dcc.Store persists it.
     *
     * Inputs : theme-toggle.n_clicks
     * State  : theme-store.data
     * Output : theme-store.data
     */
    toggleTheme: function (n_clicks, _currentStore) {
        if (!n_clicks) {
            return window.dash_clientside.no_update;
        }
        var active =
            document.documentElement.getAttribute("data-mantine-color-scheme") || "dark";
        return active === "dark" ? "light" : "dark";
    },

    /**
     * Apply the theme whenever the store value changes (including on
     * page load).  Sets the data-mantine-color-scheme attribute on
     * <html> and returns the menu-item label for the toggle.
     *
     * When storedTheme is null (first visit, nothing in localStorage)
     * the browser / OS preference is used.
     *
     * Input  : theme-store.data
     * Outputs: theme-toggle.children, theme-toggle-icon.icon,
     *          mantine-provider.forceColorScheme
     */
    applyTheme: function (storedTheme) {
        var theme;
        if (storedTheme === "light" || storedTheme === "dark") {
            theme = storedTheme;
        } else {
            var prefersDark = window.matchMedia("(prefers-color-scheme: dark)").matches;
            theme = prefersDark ? "dark" : "light";
        }

        document.documentElement.setAttribute("data-mantine-color-scheme", theme);

        // In dark mode offer "Light Mode" (sun icon); in light offer "Dark Mode" (moon icon)
        var label = theme === "dark" ? "Light Mode" : "Dark Mode";
        var icon = theme === "dark" ? "tabler:sun" : "tabler:moon";
        return [label, icon, theme];
    },
};

import importlib

from dash import Dash

from src.callbacks.theme_callbacks import register_theme_callbacks
from src.callbacks.tool_callbacks import register_tool_execution_callbacks
from src.tools.registry import create_registry


def register_callbacks(app: Dash) -> None:
    registry = create_registry()
    register_theme_callbacks(app)
    register_tool_execution_callbacks(app, registry)
    for family in registry.families():
        tab_mod = importlib.import_module(f"src.tools.{family}.tab")
        tab_mod.register_callbacks(app)
===== FILE: src/callbacks/theme_callbacks.py =====
import dash
from dash import Input, Output, State


def register_theme_callbacks(app: dash.Dash) -> None:
    # Toggle: click menu item → flip the stored theme value
    app.clientside_callback(
        "dash_clientside.theme.toggleTheme",
        Output("theme-store", "data", allow_duplicate=True),
        Input("theme-toggle", "n_clicks"),
        State("theme-store", "data"),
        prevent_initial_call=True,
    )

    # Apply: whenever the store changes (or on first load), set the
    # color-scheme attribute and update the menu item label + icon.
    app.clientside_callback(
        "dash_clientside.theme.applyTheme",
        Output("theme-toggle", "children"),
        Output("theme-toggle-icon", "icon"),
        Output("mantine-provider", "forceColorScheme"),
        Input("theme-store", "data"),
    )
===== FILE: src/callbacks/tool_callbacks.py =====
import time

from dash import Dash, Input, Output, State, callback_context, no_update

from src.components.tool_ui import format_options_display
from src.tools.registry import ToolRegistry


def register_tool_execution_callbacks(app: Dash, registry: ToolRegistry) -> None:
    for tool in registry.all_tools():
        _register_modal_callbacks(app, tool)
        _register_run_callback(app, tool)


def _register_modal_callbacks(app, tool):
    info = tool.info
    modal_id = f"{info.id}-modal"
    card_id = f"{info.id}-card"
    cancel_id = f"{info.id}-cancel-btn"
    play_id = f"{info.id}-play-btn"

    @app.callback(Output(modal_id, "opened"),
                  Input(card_id, "n_clicks"),
                  Input(play_id, "n_clicks"),
                  Input(cancel_id, "n_clicks"),
                  State(modal_id, "opened"),
                  prevent_initial_call = True)
    def toggle_modal(card_clicks, play_clicks, cancel_clicks, is_open):
        triggered = callback_context.triggered_id
        if triggered == card_id or triggered == play_id:
            return True
        if triggered == cancel_id:
            return False
        return no_update


def _register_run_callback(app, tool):
    info = tool.info
    tid = info.id

    option_inputs = [
        State(f"{tid}-opt-{opt.name}", "checked" if opt.type == "bool" else "value")
        for opt in info.options
    ]

    @app.callback(Output(f"{tid}-result-store", "data"),
                  Output(f"{tid}-result-trigger", "data"),
                  Output(f"{tid}-modal", "opened", allow_duplicate = True),
                  Output(f"{tid}-options-store", "data"),
                  Output(f"{tid}-options-display", "children"),
                  Input(f"{tid}-run-btn", "n_clicks"),
                  *option_inputs,
                  prevent_initial_call = True)
    def run_tool(n_clicks, *opt_values):
        if not n_clicks:
            return no_update, no_update, no_update, no_update, no_update

        options = {opt.name: val for opt, val in zip(info.options, opt_values, strict = True)}
        result = tool.run(None, options)

        result_data = {
            "success": result.success,
            "data": result.data,
            "error": result.error,
            "metadata": result.metadata,
        }

        trigger = {"tool_id": info.id, "timestamp": time.time()}

        return result_data, trigger, False, options, format_options_display(options)

from celery import Celery

from src.config import settings

# Create the single Celery app instance
# This MUST be created before Dash app or any tasks are imported

# Celery configuration
# Task discovery - we'll also manually ensure dash callbacks are registered by importing them in the worker entry point

===== FILE: src/celery_worker.py =====
"""
Celery worker entry point.

THIS IS THE KEY FILE THAT SOLVES THE TASK REGISTRATION PROBLEM.

When starting the Celery worker, point it at this module:
    celery -A celery_worker.celery_app worker --loglevel=info

This module imports:
1. The shared Celery app instance
2. The manual tasks module (registers @celery_app.task decorated functions)
3. The Dash app module (registers background callbacks as Celery tasks)

The order matters:
1. celery_app must exist first
2. Then tasks that depend on it can be imported
3. Dash app import triggers callback decoration, which registers them as tasks

Without importing the Dash app here, the worker would never know about
the background callbacks because they're registered dynamically when
the @callback decorators execute.
"""

# Step 1: Import the Celery app instance
from src.celery_config import celery_app

# Step 2: Import manual tasks - these use @celery_app.task decorator
#from src import tasks  # noqa: F401

# Step 3: CRITICAL - Import the Dash app to register background callbacks
# When app.dash_app is imported, it imports callbacks.py, which executes
# the @callback decorators with background=True, registering them as Celery tasks
from src.dash_app import dash_app  # noqa: F401

# The celery_app is now fully configured with all tasks registered
# Export it so the celery command can find it
__all__ = ["celery_app"]

import importlib

import dash_mantine_components as dmc
from dash import dcc, html
from dash_iconify import DashIconify

from src.components.tool_ui import (
    create_sidebar_section,
    create_tool_modals,
    create_tool_stores,
)
from src.tools.base import DataType
from src.tools.registry import create_registry

_registry = create_registry()

# Fixed tabs that always appear
_FIXED_TABS = [{"value": "tab-main-map", "label": "Main Map"},
               {"value": "tab-summary-stats", "label": "Summary Statistics"},
              ]

# Build family tabs from registry
_FAMILY_TABS = [{"value": f"tab-{family}", "label": next(t.info.tab_label for t in _registry.tools_in_family(family))} for family in _registry.families()]

MAIN_VIEW_TABS = _FIXED_TABS + _FAMILY_TABS

# Distinct background colors for each tab area (for layout verification)
TAB_COLORS = {
    "tab-main-map": "#1a3a2a",
    "tab-summary-stats": "#1a2a3a",
    "tab-dwell": "#3a1a2a",
    "tab-hotspot": "#2a2a1a",
    "tab-cotravel": "#2a1a3a",
    "tab-colocation": "#3a2a1a",
    "tab-track": "#2a3a1a",
}

def create_layout() -> html.Div:
    modals = create_tool_modals(_registry)
    stores = create_tool_stores(_registry)

    return html.Div([dcc.Store(id = "theme-store", storage_type="local"),
                     *stores,
                     dmc.MantineProvider(id = "mantine-provider",
                                         children = [*modals,
                                                     dmc.Tabs(id = "main-view-tabs",
                                                              value = "tab-main-map",
                                                              children = [dmc.Box(id = "app-container",
                                                                                  className = "app-container",
                                                                                  children = [dmc.Box(className = "banner", children = [dmc.Text("Development Environment", size = "sm")]),
                                                                                              _create_header(),
                                                                                              dmc.Box(className = "main-content", children = [_create_sidebar(), _create_tab_panels()]),
                                                                                              dmc.Box(className = "banner banner-bottom", children = [dmc.Text("Analytic App v0.1.0", size = "sm")]),
                                                                                             ]
                                                                          )
                                                                         ]
                                                      )
                                                    ]
                     )
                    ]
    )

def _create_menu() -> dmc.Menu:
    return dmc.Menu(id = "app-menu",
                    trigger = "click",
                    position = "bottom-end",
                    withArrow = True,
                    shadow = "md",
                    children = [dmc.MenuTarget(dmc.ActionIcon(dmc.Text("\u2630", size = "lg"), id = "menu-button", variant = "subtle", size = "lg", color = "gray")),
                                dmc.MenuDropdown(children = [dmc.MenuLabel("Appearance"),
                                                             dmc.MenuItem("Dark Mode", id = "theme-toggle", leftSection = DashIconify(icon = "tabler:moon", id = "theme-toggle-icon", width = 16))
                                                            ]
                                )
                               ]
    )

def _create_header() -> dmc.Box:
    return dmc.Box(className = "app-header",
                   children = [dmc.Group(className = "app-header__brand", gap = "xs", children = [dmc.Text("Analytic App", className = "app-header__logo", fw = 600, size = "lg")]),
                               dmc.TabsList(id = "main-view-tabs-list", className = "app-header__tabs", children = [dmc.TabsTab(tab["label"], value = tab["value"]) for tab in MAIN_VIEW_TABS]),
                               _create_menu()
                              ]
    )

def _create_sidebar() -> dmc.Box:
    point_cards = create_sidebar_section(_registry, DataType.POINT)
    ellipse_cards = create_sidebar_section(_registry, DataType.ELLIPSE)

    return dmc.Box(className = "sidebar",
                   children = [dmc.Box(className = "sidebar__filter", children = [dmc.Text("Filter / Graph Area", size = "sm", c = "dimmed")]),
                               dmc.Accordion(id = "sidebar-tools-accordion",
                                             multiple = True,
                                             value = ["point-tools", "ellipse-tools"],
                                             variant = "separated",
                                             className = "sidebar__accordion",
                                             children = [dmc.AccordionItem(value = "point-tools",
                                                                           className = "sidebar__section",
                                                                           children = [dmc.AccordionControl("Point-Based Tools", className = "sidebar__section-header"),
                                                                                       dmc.AccordionPanel(dmc.Stack(point_cards, gap = "xs"))
                                                                                      ]
                                                         ),
                                                         dmc.AccordionItem(value = "ellipse-tools",
                                                                           className = "sidebar__section",
                                                                           children = [dmc.AccordionControl("Ellipse-Based Tools", className = "sidebar__section-header"),
                                                                                       dmc.AccordionPanel(dmc.Stack(ellipse_cards, gap = "xs"))
                                                                                      ]
                                                         )
                                                        ]
                                )
                               ]
    )

def _create_fixed_panel(tab: dict) -> dmc.TabsPanel:
    """Create a placeholder panel for fixed tabs (Main Map, Summary Stats)."""
    return dmc.TabsPanel(value = tab["value"],
                         children = [dmc.Box(style = {"height": "100%", "backgroundColor": TAB_COLORS[tab["value"]], "display": "flex", "alignItems": "center", "justifyContent": "center"},
                                             children = [dmc.Text(tab["label"], size = "xl", c = "dimmed")]
                                     )
                                    ],
                         style = {"height": "100%"}
    )

def _create_family_panel(family: str) -> dmc.TabsPanel:
    """Create a tab panel for a tool family, using its tab.py module."""
    tab_mod = importlib.import_module(f"src.tools.{family}.tab")
    return dmc.TabsPanel(value = f"tab-{family}", children = [tab_mod.render_tab_content()], style = {"height": "100%"})

def _create_tab_panels() -> dmc.Box:
    fixed_panels = [_create_fixed_panel(tab) for tab in _FIXED_TABS]
    family_panels = [_create_family_panel(family) for family in _registry.families()]

    return dmc.Box(className = "content-area", children = fixed_panels + family_panels)
===== FILE: src/components/tool_ui.py =====
import re

import dash_mantine_components as dmc
from dash import dcc
from dash_iconify import DashIconify

from src.tools.base import BaseTool, DataType, ToolOption
from src.tools.registry import ToolRegistry


def format_options_display(options: dict) -> str:
    """Format an options dict as a human-readable summary string.

    {"min_duration_minutes": 30, "max_radius_meters": 100} ->
    "Min Duration Minutes: 30 | Max Radius Meters: 100"

    Returns "Default settings" for empty dict.
    """
    if not options:
        return "Default settings"
    parts = []
    for key, value in options.items():
        label = key.replace("_", " ").title()
        parts.append(f"{label}: {value}")
    return " | ".join(parts)


def _get_initials(name: str) -> str:
    """Extract up to 2 uppercase initials from a tool name, stripping parentheses.

    "Dwell (Point)" -> "DP"
    "Hotspot" -> "H"
    """
    cleaned = re.sub(r"[()]", "", name)
    words = cleaned.split()
    initials = "".join(w[0].upper() for w in words if w)
    return initials[:2]


def _option_to_input(
    opt: ToolOption, tool_id: str
) -> dmc.NumberInput | dmc.Switch | dmc.Select | dmc.TextInput:
    """Convert a ToolOption to the appropriate Mantine input component."""
    input_id = f"{tool_id}-opt-{opt.name}"

    if opt.type == "int":
        control = dmc.NumberInput(
            id=input_id,
            label=opt.label,
            description=opt.description or None,
            value=opt.default,
            min=opt.min_value,
            max=opt.max_value,
            step=int(opt.step) if opt.step else 1,
        )
    elif opt.type == "float":
        control = dmc.NumberInput(
            id=input_id,
            label=opt.label,
            description=opt.description or None,
            value=opt.default,
            min=opt.min_value,
            max=opt.max_value,
            step=opt.step if opt.step else 0.1,
            decimalScale=5,
        )
    elif opt.type == "bool":
        control = dmc.Switch(
            id=input_id,
            label=opt.label,
            description=opt.description or None,
            checked=opt.default,
        )
    elif opt.type == "select":
        control = dmc.Select(
            id=input_id,
            label=opt.label,
            description=opt.description or None,
            value=opt.default,
            data=opt.choices or [],
        )
    else:
        control = dmc.TextInput(
            id=input_id,
            label=opt.label,
            description=opt.description or None,
            value=opt.default or "",
        )

    return control


def _create_tool_card(tool: BaseTool) -> dmc.Box:
    """Create a sidebar card for a single tool."""
    info = tool.info
    tid = info.id
    initials = _get_initials(info.name)
    defaults = {opt.name: opt.default for opt in info.options}
    default_display = format_options_display(defaults)

    return dmc.Box(
        id=f"{tid}-card",
        className="sidebar__tool-card",
        style={"cursor": "pointer"},
        children=[
            dmc.Stack(
                gap="xs",
                children=[
                    dmc.Group(
                        wrap="nowrap",
                        align="center",
                        children=[
                            dmc.Avatar(
                                initials,
                                color="blue",
                                size="md",
                                radius="xl",
                            ),
                            dmc.Stack(
                                gap=4,
                                style={"flex": 1, "minWidth": 0},
                                children=[
                                    dmc.Text(
                                        info.name,
                                        size="sm",
                                        fw=600,
                                        truncate="end",
                                    ),
                                    dmc.Text(
                                        default_display,
                                        id=f"{tid}-options-display",
                                        size="xs",
                                        c="dimmed",
                                        truncate="end",
                                    ),
                                ],
                            ),
                            dmc.ActionIcon(
                                DashIconify(icon="ic:round-play-arrow", width=20),
                                id=f"{tid}-play-btn",
                                variant="filled",
                                color="green",
                                size="lg",
                                radius="xl",
                            ),
                        ],
                    ),
                    dmc.Box(
                        id=f"{tid}-progress-container",
                        style={"display": "none"},
                        children=[
                            dmc.Progress(
                                id=f"{tid}-progress-bar",
                                value=0,
                                size="sm",
                                striped=True,
                                animated=True,
                            ),
                        ],
                    ),
                ],
            ),
        ],
    )


def create_sidebar_section(registry: ToolRegistry, data_type: DataType) -> list:
    """Generate tool cards for a sidebar accordion section."""
    return [_create_tool_card(t) for t in registry.tools_for_data_type(data_type)]


def _create_modal(tool: BaseTool) -> dmc.Modal:
    """Create a configuration modal for a single tool."""
    info = tool.info
    option_inputs = [_option_to_input(opt, info.id) for opt in info.options]

    return dmc.Modal(
        id=f"{info.id}-modal",
        title=info.name,
        centered=True,
        children=[
            dmc.Text(info.description, size="sm", c="dimmed", mb="md"),
            dmc.Stack(gap="sm", children=option_inputs),
            dmc.Group(
                justify="flex-end",
                mt="lg",
                children=[
                    dmc.Button(
                        "Cancel",
                        id=f"{info.id}-cancel-btn",
                        variant="subtle",
                        color="gray",
                    ),
                    dmc.Button(
                        "Run",
                        id=f"{info.id}-run-btn",
                        variant="filled",
                    ),
                ],
            ),
        ],
    )


def create_tool_modals(registry: ToolRegistry) -> list[dmc.Modal]:
    """Generate one modal per tool (10 total)."""
    return [_create_modal(t) for t in registry.all_tools()]


def create_tool_stores(registry: ToolRegistry) -> list[dcc.Store]:
    """Generate per-tool stores: options, result, and result-trigger."""
    stores: list[dcc.Store] = []
    for tool in registry.all_tools():
        tid = tool.info.id
        defaults = {opt.name: opt.default for opt in tool.info.options}
        stores.extend(
            [
                dcc.Store(id=f"{tid}-options-store", data=defaults),
                dcc.Store(id=f"{tid}-result-store"),
                dcc.Store(id=f"{tid}-result-trigger"),
            ]
        )
    return stores
===== FILE: src/config.py =====
from functools import lru_cache

from pydantic import Field
from pydantic_settings import BaseSettings, SettingsConfigDict


class ServerSettings(BaseSettings):
    model_config = SettingsConfigDict(env_prefix="APP_")

    host: str = Field(default="0.0.0.0", description="Server host")
    port: int = Field(default=8050, description="Server port")
    debug: bool = Field(default=False, description="Debug mode")
    reload: bool = Field(default=False, description="Auto-reload on code changes")


class RedisSettings(BaseSettings):
    model_config = SettingsConfigDict(env_prefix="REDIS_")

    broker_url: str = Field(default="redis://localhost:6379/0", description="Redis URL for Celery broker")
    backend_url: str = Field(default="redis://localhost:6379/1", description="Redis URL for Celery result backend")


class Settings(BaseSettings):
    model_config = SettingsConfigDict(env_file=".env", env_file_encoding="utf-8", extra="ignore")
    server: ServerSettings = Field(default_factory=ServerSettings)
    redis: RedisSettings = Field(default_factory=RedisSettings)


@lru_cache
def get_settings() -> Settings:
    return Settings()


settings = get_settings()

===== FILE: src/tools/base.py =====
from __future__ import annotations

from abc import ABC, abstractmethod
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from enum import Enum
from typing import Any


class DataType(Enum):
    POINT = "point"
    ELLIPSE = "ellipse"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ToolOption:
    name: str
    label: str
    type: str  # "int" | "float" | "bool" | "select" | "str"
    default: Any
    description: str = ""
    min_value: float | None = None
    max_value: float | None = None
    step: float | None = None
    choices: list[dict] | None = None


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ToolInfo:
    id: str
    family: str
    name: str
    description: str
    icon: str
    data_type: DataType
    tab_label: str
    options: list[ToolOption] = field(default_factory=list)


@dataclass
class ToolResult:
    success: bool
    data: Any = None
    geojson: dict | None = None
    error: str | None = None
    metadata: dict = field(default_factory=dict)


class BaseTool(ABC):
    @property
    @abstractmethod
    def info(self) -> ToolInfo: ...

    @abstractmethod
    def run(self, data: Any, options: dict[str, Any]) -> ToolResult: ...

    def validate_options(self, options: dict) -> dict:
        defaults = {opt.name: opt.default for opt in self.info.options}
        return {**defaults, **options}

    def to_geojson(self, result: ToolResult) -> dict:
        return result.geojson or {"type": "FeatureCollection", "features": []}

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any

from src.tools.base import BaseTool, DataType, ToolInfo, ToolOption, ToolResult


class EllipseColocationTool(BaseTool):
    @property
    def info(self) -> ToolInfo:
        return ToolInfo(
            id="ellipse-colocation",
            family="colocation",
            name="Colocation (Ellipse)",
            description="Find colocation events using error ellipse intersection",
            icon="tabler:map-pin-share",
            data_type=DataType.ELLIPSE,
            tab_label="Colocation Results",
            options=[
                ToolOption(
                    name="intersection_threshold",
                    label="Intersection Threshold",
                    type="float",
                    default=0.2,
                    min_value=0.0,
                    max_value=1.0,
                    step=0.05,
                ),
                ToolOption(
                    name="time_threshold_minutes",
                    label="Time Threshold (min)",
                    type="int",
                    default=10,
                    min_value=1,
                    max_value=60,
                ),
            ],
        )

    def run(self, data: Any, options: dict[str, Any]) -> ToolResult:
        options = self.validate_options(options)
        return ToolResult(
            success=True,
            data=None,
            metadata={"tool": "ellipse-colocation", "options": options},
        )

===== FILE: src/tools/registry.py =====
from __future__ import annotations

import importlib

from src.tools.base import BaseTool, DataType

TOOL_MODULES = [
    "src.tools.dwell",
    "src.tools.hotspot",
    "src.tools.cotravel",
    "src.tools.colocation",
    "src.tools.track",
]


class ToolRegistry:
    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self._tools: dict[str, BaseTool] = {}
        self._order: list[str] = []

    def register(self, tool: BaseTool) -> None:
        tid = tool.info.id
        if tid in self._tools:
            msg = f"Duplicate tool id: {tid}"
            raise ValueError(msg)
        self._tools[tid] = tool
        self._order.append(tid)

    def get(self, tool_id: str) -> BaseTool | None:
        return self._tools.get(tool_id)

    def all_tools(self) -> list[BaseTool]:
        return [self._tools[tid] for tid in self._order]

    def tools_for_data_type(self, dt: DataType) -> list[BaseTool]:
        return [t for t in self.all_tools() if t.info.data_type == dt]

    def families(self) -> list[str]:
        seen: set[str] = set()
        result: list[str] = []
        for t in self.all_tools():
            if t.info.family not in seen:
                seen.add(t.info.family)
                result.append(t.info.family)
        return result

    def tools_in_family(self, family: str) -> list[BaseTool]:
        return [t for t in self.all_tools() if t.info.family == family]


def create_registry() -> ToolRegistry:
    registry = ToolRegistry()
    for module_path in TOOL_MODULES:
        mod = importlib.import_module(module_path)
        for tool in mod.create_tools():
            registry.register(tool)
    return registry

                ),

                    name="confidence_level",
                    label="Confidence Level",
                    type="float",
                    default=0.95,
                    min_value=0.5,
                    max_value=1.0,
                    step=0.05,
                ),
            ],
        )

    def run(self, data: Any, options: dict[str, Any]) -> ToolResult:
        options = self.validate_options(options)
        return ToolResult(
            success=True,
            data=None,
            metadata={"tool": "ellipse-track", "options": options},
        )

===== FILE: tests/conftest.py =====
import os
import tempfile
import threading
import time
from collections.abc import Generator

import pytest
from selenium import webdriver
from selenium.webdriver.chrome.options import Options
from selenium.webdriver.chrome.service import Service

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Paths — adjust these if your environment differs
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
CHROMIUM_BINARY = "/snap/bin/chromium"
CHROMEDRIVER_BINARY = os.path.join(
    os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(__file__)), ".venv", "bin", "chromedriver"
)
APP_HOST = "127.0.0.1"
APP_PORT = 18050  # Non-standard port to avoid conflicts with a running dev server


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Selenium fixtures
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


def _make_chrome_options() -> Options:
    """Build Chrome options for headless WSL2 + snap Chromium."""
    options = Options()
    options.binary_location = CHROMIUM_BINARY
    options.add_argument("--headless=new")
    options.add_argument("--no-sandbox")
    options.add_argument("--disable-dev-shm-usage")
    options.add_argument("--disable-gpu")
    options.add_argument("--disable-software-rasterizer")
    options.add_argument("--remote-debugging-port=9222")
    options.add_argument(f"--user-data-dir={tempfile.mkdtemp()}")
    options.set_capability("goog:loggingPrefs", {"browser": "SEVERE"})
    return options


@pytest.fixture
def browser() -> Generator[webdriver.Chrome, None, None]:
    """Per-test Chrome browser instance."""
    service = Service(CHROMEDRIVER_BINARY)
    driver = webdriver.Chrome(service=service, options=_make_chrome_options())
    yield driver
    driver.quit()


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Dash server fixture
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


@pytest.fixture(scope="session")
def dash_server() -> Generator[str, None, None]:
    """
    Start the Dash app in a background thread for the test session.

    Yields the base URL (e.g. "http://127.0.0.1:18050").
    """
    from src.dash_app import create_dash_app

    app = create_dash_app()

    server_thread = threading.Thread(
        target=app.run,
        kwargs={"host": APP_HOST, "port": APP_PORT, "debug": False},
        daemon=True,
    )
    server_thread.start()

    # Wait for server to be ready
    base_url = f"http://{APP_HOST}:{APP_PORT}"
    _wait_for_server(base_url)

    yield base_url


def _wait_for_server(url: str, timeout: float = 10.0) -> None:
    """Poll until the server responds or timeout."""
    import urllib.request

    deadline = time.monotonic() + timeout
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        try:
            urllib.request.urlopen(url, timeout=1)
            return
        except Exception:
            time.sleep(0.2)
    raise RuntimeError(f"Dash server did not start within {timeout}s at {url}")
===== FILE: tests/test_ui_shell.py =====
"""UI tests for the shell layout.

These tests start the Dash app in a thread and use Selenium to verify
that the rendered page matches expectations.

Marked with @pytest.mark.ui — skip with: pytest -m "not ui"
"""

import pytest
from selenium.webdriver.common.by import By
from selenium.webdriver.support import expected_conditions as EC
from selenium.webdriver.support.ui import WebDriverWait

from src.components.layout import MAIN_VIEW_TABS


@pytest.mark.ui
class TestShellUI:
    """Browser-based tests for the shell layout."""

    @pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
    def _load_app(self, browser, dash_server):
        """Navigate to the app before each test."""
        self.driver = browser
        self.wait = WebDriverWait(browser, 5)
        browser.get(dash_server)
        # Wait for the app container to render
        self.wait.until(EC.presence_of_element_located((By.CLASS_NAME, "app-container")))

    def test_header_renders(self):
        """App header with logo text should be visible."""
        logo = self.driver.find_element(By.CLASS_NAME, "app-header__logo")
        assert logo.text == "Analytic App"

    def test_all_tabs_visible(self):
        """All five tab labels should be rendered in the header."""
        for tab in MAIN_VIEW_TABS:
            # Mantine Tabs renders: id="<tabs-id>-tab-<value>"
            elem = self.driver.find_element(By.ID, f"main-view-tabs-tab-{tab['value']}")
            assert elem.is_displayed()

    def test_tab_switching(self):
        """Clicking a tab should activate it."""
        tab = self.driver.find_element(By.ID, "main-view-tabs-tab-tab-dwell")
        tab.click()
        self.wait.until(
            EC.presence_of_element_located(
                (By.CSS_SELECTOR, "#main-view-tabs-tab-tab-dwell[data-active]")
            )
        )

    def test_hamburger_menu_opens(self):
        """Clicking the hamburger icon should reveal the theme toggle."""
        menu_btn = self.driver.find_element(By.ID, "menu-button")
        menu_btn.click()
        toggle = self.wait.until(EC.visibility_of_element_located((By.ID, "theme-toggle")))
        assert toggle.text in ("Light Mode", "Dark Mode")

    def test_banners_visible(self):
        """Top and bottom banners should be rendered."""
        banners = self.driver.find_elements(By.CLASS_NAME, "banner")
        assert len(banners) == 2
        assert "Development Environment" in banners[0].text
        assert "Analytic App v0.1.0" in banners[1].text

    def test_sidebar_visible(self):
        """Sidebar div should be rendered and visible."""
        sidebar = self.driver.find_element(By.CLASS_NAME, "sidebar")
        assert sidebar.is_displayed()

    def test_no_browser_console_errors(self):
        """Browser console should have no severe errors on initial load."""
        logs = self.driver.get_log("browser")
        severe = [log for log in logs if log["level"] == "SEVERE"]
        assert severe == [], f"Browser console errors: {severe}"

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Theme toggle tests
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def _get_html_color_scheme(self):
        """Read the data-mantine-color-scheme attribute from the <html> element."""
        return self.driver.find_element(By.TAG_NAME, "html").get_attribute(
            "data-mantine-color-scheme"
        )

    def _open_menu_and_click_toggle(self):
        """Open the hamburger menu and click the theme toggle."""
        menu_btn = self.driver.find_element(By.ID, "menu-button")
        menu_btn.click()
        toggle = self.wait.until(EC.visibility_of_element_located((By.ID, "theme-toggle")))
        toggle.click()
        # Brief wait for clientside callback to execute
        import time

        time.sleep(0.3)

    def test_theme_toggle_changes_color_scheme(self):
        """Clicking the theme toggle should flip dark <-> light on the html element."""
        initial_scheme = self._get_html_color_scheme()
        expected_after_toggle = "dark" if initial_scheme == "light" else "light"

        self._open_menu_and_click_toggle()

        new_scheme = self._get_html_color_scheme()
        assert new_scheme == expected_after_toggle, (
            f"Expected '{expected_after_toggle}' after toggle but got '{new_scheme}'"
        )

    def test_theme_toggle_label_updates(self):
        """After toggling, the menu item label should reflect the opposite action."""
        # Toggle the theme
        self._open_menu_and_click_toggle()

        new_scheme = self._get_html_color_scheme()

        # Re-open the menu to check the label
        menu_btn = self.driver.find_element(By.ID, "menu-button")
        menu_btn.click()
        toggle = self.wait.until(EC.visibility_of_element_located((By.ID, "theme-toggle")))

        # In dark mode the label offers "Light Mode", in light it offers "Dark Mode"
        if new_scheme == "dark":
            assert toggle.text == "Light Mode", f"Expected 'Light Mode' but got '{toggle.text}'"
        else:
            assert toggle.text == "Dark Mode", f"Expected 'Dark Mode' but got '{toggle.text}'"

    def test_theme_persists_after_reload(self):
        """Theme preference should persist via localStorage across page reloads."""
        initial_scheme = self._get_html_color_scheme()
        target_scheme = "dark" if initial_scheme == "light" else "light"

        # Toggle to the opposite theme
        self._open_menu_and_click_toggle()

        # Verify it changed
        assert self._get_html_color_scheme() == target_scheme

        # Reload the page
        self.driver.refresh()
        self.wait.until(EC.presence_of_element_located((By.CLASS_NAME, "app-container")))

        # Brief wait for _theme_init.js to run and Dash to render
        import time

        time.sleep(0.5)

        # Verify the theme survived the reload
        persisted_scheme = self._get_html_color_scheme()
        assert persisted_scheme == target_scheme, (
            f"Expected '{target_scheme}' after reload but got '{persisted_scheme}'"
        )

    def test_theme_double_toggle_returns_to_original(self):
        """Toggling twice should return to the original theme."""
        initial_scheme = self._get_html_color_scheme()

        # First toggle
        self._open_menu_and_click_toggle()
        assert self._get_html_color_scheme() != initial_scheme

        # Second toggle
        self._open_menu_and_click_toggle()
        assert self._get_html_color_scheme() == initial_scheme

class TestServerSettings:

        monkeypatch.delenv("APP_PORT", raising=False)
        monkeypatch.delenv("APP_DEBUG", raising=False)
        monkeypatch.delenv("APP_RELOAD", raising=False)
        s = ServerSettings()

        assert s.port == 8050
        assert s.debug is False
        assert s.reload is False

    def test_env_override(self, monkeypatch):

        monkeypatch.setenv("APP_PORT", "9000")
        s = ServerSettings()
        assert s.debug is True
        assert s.port == 9000

===== FILE: tests/test_unit_layout.py =====
import pytest

from src.components.layout import MAIN_VIEW_TABS, TAB_COLORS, create_layout


def _find_by_id(component, target_id):
    """Recursively search a Dash component tree for a component with the given id."""
    comp_id = getattr(component, "id", None)
    if comp_id == target_id:
        return component
    children = getattr(component, "children", None)
    if children is None:
        return None
    if not isinstance(children, (list, tuple)):
        children = [children]
    for child in children:
        if hasattr(child, "id") or hasattr(child, "children"):
            result = _find_by_id(child, target_id)
            if result is not None:
                return result
    return None


@pytest.mark.unit
class TestLayoutStructure:
    """Verify the layout tree has the right pieces."""

    @pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
    def _build_layout(self):
        self.layout = create_layout()

    def test_tabs_all_enabled(self):
        """All tabs should have no disabled flag."""
        for tab in MAIN_VIEW_TABS:
            assert "disabled" not in tab or tab.get("disabled") is False

    def test_tabs_have_unique_colors(self):
        """Each tab should map to a distinct background color."""
        colors = list(TAB_COLORS.values())
        assert len(colors) == len(set(colors)), "Tab colors must be unique"

    def test_seven_tabs_defined(self):
        assert len(MAIN_VIEW_TABS) == 7

    def test_layout_returns_div(self):
        """Top-level element should be an html.Div."""
        from dash import html

        assert isinstance(self.layout, html.Div)

    def test_theme_store_present(self):
        """Layout should contain a dcc.Store with id='theme-store' and local storage."""
        from dash import dcc

        store = _find_by_id(self.layout, "theme-store")
        assert store is not None, "dcc.Store with id='theme-store' not found in layout"
        assert isinstance(store, dcc.Store)
        assert store.storage_type == "local"  # type: ignore[attr-defined]

    def test_theme_toggle_present(self):
        """Layout should contain a menu item with id='theme-toggle'."""
        toggle = _find_by_id(self.layout, "theme-toggle")
        assert toggle is not None, "Menu item with id='theme-toggle' not found in layout"

    def test_mantine_provider_present(self):
        """Layout should contain a MantineProvider with id='mantine-provider'."""
        import dash_mantine_components as dmc

        provider = _find_by_id(self.layout, "mantine-provider")
        assert provider is not None, "MantineProvider with id='mantine-provider' not found"
        assert isinstance(provider, dmc.MantineProvider)

            "ellipse-dwell",

            "ellipse-track",

            assert tool is not None
            assert tool.info.id == tool_id

        """get() should return None for unknown tool ID."""
        tool = self.registry.get("unknown-tool")
        assert tool is None

    def test_get_empty_string_returns_none(self):
        """get() should return None for empty string."""

        assert tool is None

@pytest.mark.unit
class TestToolRegistryRegister:
    """Test the registry.register() method."""

    def test_register_duplicate_id_raises_value_error(self):
        """Registering a duplicate tool ID should raise ValueError."""
        from src.tools.dwell.point import PointDwellTool

        registry = ToolRegistry()
        tool1 = PointDwellTool()
        tool2 = PointDwellTool()

        registry.register(tool1)
        with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="Duplicate tool id"):
            registry.register(tool2)

    def test_register_preserves_order(self):
        """Tools should be returned in registration order."""
        from src.tools.dwell.point import PointDwellTool
        from src.tools.hotspot.point import PointHotspotTool
        from src.tools.track.point import PointTrackTool

        registry = ToolRegistry()
        tool1 = PointDwellTool()
        tool2 = PointHotspotTool()
        tool3 = PointTrackTool()

        registry.register(tool1)
        registry.register(tool2)
        registry.register(tool3)

        tools = registry.all_tools()
        assert tools[0].info.id == "point-dwell"
        assert tools[1].info.id == "point-hotspot"
        assert tools[2].info.id == "point-track"
